```
1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→# Marker constants used by CLI and gateway to tag auto-reply messages.
    12→GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX = "autoreply-"
    13→CLI_INPUT_PREFIX = "[autoreply]"
    14→
    15→
    16→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    17→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    18→
    19→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    20→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    21→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    22→
    23→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    24→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    25→    """
    26→    # /autoreply off
    27→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    28→        return "off", None
    29→
    30→    # /autoreply max <N>
    31→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    32→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    33→        if len(parts) == 2:
    34→            try:
    35→                n = int(parts[1])
    36→                if n < 1:
    37→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    38→                return f"max:{n}", None
    39→            except ValueError:
    40→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    41→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    42→
    43→    # /autoreply (no args)
    44→    if not args:
    45→        return "status", None
    46→
    47→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    48→    literal = False
    49→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    50→    remaining_parts = []
    51→
    52→    parts = args.split()
    53→    i = 0
    54→    while i < len(parts):
    55→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    56→            literal = True
    57→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    58→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    59→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    60→            i += 1
    61→            try:
    62→                n = int(parts[i])
    63→                if n < 1:
    64→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    65→                max_turns = n
    66→            except ValueError:
    67→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    68→        else:
    69→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    70→        i += 1
    71→
    72→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    73→
    74→    if literal:
    75→        if not prompt:
    76→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    77→        return "enabled", {
    78→            "prompt": prompt,
    79→            "model": None,
    80→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    81→            "turn_count": 0,
    82→            "literal": True,
    83→        }
    84→
    85→    if not prompt:
    86→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    87→
    88→    return "enabled", {
    89→        "prompt": prompt,
    90→        "model": None,
    91→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    92→        "turn_count": 0,
    93→    }
    94→
    95→
    96→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    97→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    98→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    99→
   100→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
   101→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
   102→    """
   103→    recent = [
   104→        m for m in (history or [])
   105→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   106→    ][-20:]
   107→
   108→    system_prompt = (
   109→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   110→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   111→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   112→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   113→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   114→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   115→    )
   116→
   117→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   118→    for m in recent:
   119→        content = m["content"]
   120→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   121→        if isinstance(content, list):
   122→            content = "\n".join(
   123→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   124→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   125→            )
   126→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   127→    messages.append({
   128→        "role": "user",
   129→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   130→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   131→    })
   132→    return messages
   133→
   134→
   135→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   136→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   137→
   138→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   139→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   140→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   141→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   142→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   143→
   144→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   145→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   146→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   147→
   148→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   149→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   150→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   151→    """
   152→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   153→        return None, True
   154→
   155→    if config.get("literal"):
   156→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   157→        return config["prompt"], False
   158→
   159→    return None, False
   160→
   161→
   162→def prepare_llm_call(config: Dict[str, Any],
   163→                     history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   164→    """Build call_llm kwargs from config and history."""
   165→    messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
   166→    kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   167→        "task": "autoreply",
   168→        "messages": messages,
   169→        "temperature": 0.7,
   170→        "max_tokens": 1024,
   171→    }
   172→    if config.get("model"):
   173→        kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
   174→    return kwargs
   175→
   176→
   177→def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
   178→    """Extract reply text from LLM response. Returns text or None."""
   179→    if not response.choices:
   180→        return None
   181→    content = response.choices[0].message.content
   182→    if not content:
   183→        return None
   184→    config["turn_count"] += 1
   185→    return content.strip()
   186→
   187→
   188→def session_info(config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]) -> dict:
   189→    """Build autoreply status dict for the control API."""
   190→    return {
   191→        "autoreply": {
   192→            "enabled": config is not None,
   193→            "prompt": config.get("prompt", "") if config else None,
   194→            "max_turns": config.get("max_turns", 0) if config else None,
   195→            "turn_count": config.get("turn_count", 0) if config else None,
   196→        },
   197→    }
   198→
   199→
   200→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   201→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   202→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   203→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   204→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   205→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   206→        prompt_preview += "..."
   207→    return (
   208→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   209→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   210→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   211→    )
   212→
```

```
    74→def _load_prefill_messages(file_path: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    75→    """Load ephemeral prefill messages from a JSON file.
    76→    
    77→    The file should contain a JSON array of {role, content} dicts, e.g.:
    78→        [{"role": "user", "content": "Hi"}, {"role": "assistant", "content": "Hello!"}]
    79→    
    80→    Relative paths are resolved from ~/.hermes/.
    81→    Returns an empty list if the path is empty or the file doesn't exist.
    82→    """
    83→    if not file_path:
    84→        return []
    85→    path = Path(file_path).expanduser()
    86→    if not path.is_absolute():
    87→        path = Path.home() / ".hermes" / path
    88→    if not path.exists():
    89→        logger.warning("Prefill messages file not found: %s", path)
    90→        return []
    91→    try:
    92→        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    93→            data = json.load(f)
    94→        if not isinstance(data, list):
    95→            logger.warning("Prefill messages file must contain a JSON array: %s", path)
    96→            return []
    97→        return data
    98→    except Exception as e:
    99→        logger.warning("Failed to load prefill messages from %s: %s", path, e)
   100→        return []
```

```
  2983→            _cprint("  Send a message to start the loop. Use /autoreply off to stop.")
```

```
   315→    def test_turn_counter_reset_on_real_message(self):
```

```
   981→    """Adapter between ControlAPI and HermesCLI.
   982→
   983→    Exposes the same interface that ControlAPI expects from GatewayRunner,
   984→    bridging the sync/single-session CLI with the async ControlAPI server.

   989→        self._cli = cli_instance

   991→    def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True):
   992→        """Inject a message into the CLI's input queues."""
   993→        if interrupt and self._cli._agent_running:
   994→            self._cli._interrupt_queue.put(text)
   995→        else:
   996→            self._cli._pending_input.put(text)

  1000→        from agent.autoreply import session_info
  1001→        cfg = getattr(self._cli, "_autoreply_config", None)
  1002→        return session_info(cfg)
```

```
1470→    
  1471→    def _start_control_api(self):
  1472→        """Start the control API in a background thread for external model switching."""
  1473→        try:
  1474→            import asyncio as _aio
  1475→            import threading
  1476→            from gateway.control_api import ControlAPI
  1477→
  1478→            shim = _CLIRunner(self.agent, self.session_id, self)
  1479→            api = ControlAPI(shim)
  1480→            ready = threading.Event()
  1481→
  1482→            def _run():
  1483→                try:
  1484→                    loop = _aio.new_event_loop()
  1485→                    _aio.set_event_loop(loop)
  1486→                    loop.run_until_complete(api.start())
  1487→                    ready.set()
  1488→                    loop.run_forever()
  1489→                except Exception as e:
  1490→                    logger.error("Control API thread crashed: %s", e)
  1491→                    ready.set()  # Unblock caller even on failure
  1492→
  1493→            t = threading.Thread(target=_run, daemon=True, name="control-api")
  1494→            t.start()
  1495→            ready.wait(timeout=5.0)
  1496→        except Exception as e:
  1497→            logger.debug("Control API failed to start: %s", e)
```

```
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.

     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→# Marker constants used by CLI and gateway to tag auto-reply messages.

    13→CLI_INPUT_PREFIX = "[autoreply]"
    14→
    15→
    16→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    17→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    18→
    19→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    20→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    21→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    22→
    23→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    24→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    25→    """
    26→    # /autoreply off
    27→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    28→        return "off", None
    29→
    30→    # /autoreply max <N>
    31→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    32→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    33→        if len(parts) == 2:
    34→            try:
    35→                n = int(parts[1])
    36→                if n < 1:
    37→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    38→                return f"max:{n}", None
    39→            except ValueError:
    40→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    41→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    42→
    43→    # /autoreply (no args)
    44→    if not args:
    45→        return "status", None
    46→
    47→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    48→    literal = False
    49→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    50→    remaining_parts = []
    51→
    52→    parts = args.split()
    53→    i = 0
    54→    while i < len(parts):
    55→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    56→            literal = True
    57→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    58→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    59→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    60→            i += 1
    61→            try:
    62→                n = int(parts[i])
    63→                if n < 1:
    64→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    65→                max_turns = n
    66→            except ValueError:
    67→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    68→        else:
    69→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    70→        i += 1
    71→
    72→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    73→
    74→    if literal:
    75→        if not prompt:
    76→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    77→        return "enabled", {
    78→            "prompt": prompt,
    79→            "model": None,
    80→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    81→            "turn_count": 0,
    82→            "literal": True,
    83→        }
    84→
    85→    if not prompt:
    86→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    87→
    88→    return "enabled", {
    89→        "prompt": prompt,
    90→        "model": None,
    91→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    92→        "turn_count": 0,
    93→    }
```

```
    74→def _load_prefill_messages(file_path: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    75→    """Load ephemeral prefill messages from a JSON file.
    76→    
    77→    The file should contain a JSON array of {role, content} dicts, e.g.:
    78→        [{"role": "user", "content": "Hi"}, {"role": "assistant", "content": "Hello!"}]
    79→    
    80→    Relative paths are resolved from ~/.hermes/.
    81→    Returns an empty list if the path is empty or the file doesn't exist.
    82→    """
    83→    if not file_path:
    84→        return []
    85→    path = Path(file_path).expanduser()
    86→    if not path.is_absolute():
    87→        path = Path.home() / ".hermes" / path
    88→    if not path.exists():
    89→        logger.warning("Prefill messages file not found: %s", path)
    90→        return []
    91→    try:
    92→        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    93→            data = json.load(f)
    94→        if not isinstance(data, list):
    95→            logger.warning("Prefill messages file must contain a JSON array: %s", path)
    96→            return []
    97→        return data
    98→    except Exception as e:
    99→        logger.warning("Failed to load prefill messages from %s: %s", path, e)
   100→        return []
```

```
   293→# ── CLI_INPUT_PREFIX usage tests ─────────────────────────────────────────
   294→
   295→
   296→class TestCLIInputPrefix:
   297→    """Tests for CLI_INPUT_PREFIX constant usage in the input loop."""
   298→
   299→    def test_prefix_value(self):
   300→        assert CLI_INPUT_PREFIX == "[autoreply]"
   301→
   302→    def test_prefix_stripping(self):
   303→        """Simulate the prefix-stripping logic from the CLI input loop."""
   304→        user_input = f"{CLI_INPUT_PREFIX}What about types?"
   305→        if user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   306→            user_input = user_input[len(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):]
   307→        assert user_input == "What about types?"
   308→
   309→    def test_real_message_does_not_strip(self):
   310→        user_input = "What about types?"
   311→        if user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   312→            user_input = user_input[len(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):]
   313→        assert user_input == "What about types?"
   314→
   315→    def test_turn_counter_reset_on_real_message(self):
   316→        """Real messages reset turn counter; prefixed messages don't."""
   317→        config = {"turn_count": 5}
   318→
   319→        # Real message resets
   320→        user_input = "hello"
   321→        if not user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   322→            config["turn_count"] = 0
   323→        assert config["turn_count"] == 0
   324→
   325→        # Autoreply message does not reset
   326→        config["turn_count"] = 5
   327→        user_input = f"{CLI_INPUT_PREFIX}generated reply"
   328→        if not user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   329→            config["turn_count"] = 0
   330→        assert config["turn_count"] == 5
   331→
```

```
   579→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   580→# Turn counter reset & auto-reply label in _handle_message
   581→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   582→
   583→
   584→class TestAutoreplyInHandleMessage:
   585→    """Tests for auto-reply wiring in _handle_message.
   586→
   587→    These test the auto-reply logic directly rather than going through the
   588→    full _handle_message pipeline, which requires too much setup. The
   589→    individual methods (_generate_autoreply, _process_autoreply) are
   590→    thoroughly tested above; here we verify the wiring code itself.
   591→    """
   592→
   593→    def test_autoreply_message_detected_by_message_id(self):
   594→        """Messages with autoreply- prefix in message_id are detected."""
   595→        event = _make_event(text="test", message_id="autoreply-123.456")
   596→        assert (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   597→
   598→        event2 = _make_event(text="test", message_id="regular-123")
   599→        assert not (event2.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   600→
   601→    def test_turn_counter_reset_logic(self):
   602→        """Real messages reset counter; autoreply messages don't."""
   603→        runner = _make_runner()
   604→        session_key = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
   605→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   606→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 7,
   607→        }
   608→
   609→        # Real message — should reset
   610→        is_autoreply = ("regular-msg" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   611→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   612→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   613→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 0
   614→
   615→        # Restore and test autoreply message — should NOT reset
   616→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 7
   617→        is_autoreply = ("autoreply-123.456" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   618→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   619→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   620→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 7
   621→
   622→    def test_response_labeling_logic(self):
   623→        """Auto-reply responses get labeled with turn count."""
   624→        runner = _make_runner()
   625→        session_key = "test_key"
   626→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   627→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 3,
   628→        }
   629→
   630→        # Simulate the labeling code from _handle_message
   631→        is_autoreply = True
   632→        response = "Agent response here"
   633→        event_text = "Generated question"
   634→
   635→        if is_autoreply and response:
   636→            ar_config = runner._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
   637→            if ar_config:
   638→                response = (
   639→                    f"**[Auto-reply {ar_config['turn_count']}/{ar_config['max_turns']}]:** "
   640→                    f"{event_text}\n\n---\n\n{response}"
   641→                )
   642→
   643→        assert f"**[Auto-reply 3/{_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS}]:**" in response
   644→        assert "Generated question" in response
   645→        assert "Agent response here" in response
   646→
   647→    def test_no_label_for_regular_messages(self):
   648→        """Regular messages are not labeled."""
   649→        response = "Agent response"
   650→        is_autoreply = False
   651→
   652→        if is_autoreply and response:
   653→            response = "SHOULD NOT HAPPEN"
   654→
   655→        assert response == "Agent response"
   656→
```

```
   142→
   143→@pytest.mark.asyncio
   144→async def test_missing_header_returns_403():
   145→    """Requests without X-Hermes-Control header should be rejected."""
   146→    api, _ = _make_control_api()
   147→
   148→    from aiohttp.test_utils import TestClient, TestServer
   149→    async with TestClient(TestServer(api.app)) as client:
   150→        resp = await client.get("/health")
   151→        assert resp.status == 403
   152→        body = await resp.json()

   162→    async with TestClient(TestServer(api.app)) as client:
   163→        resp = await client.get("/health",
   164→                                headers={REQUIRED_HEADER: "1"})
   165→        assert resp.status == 200
   166→        body = await resp.json()
   167→        assert body["status"] == "ok"
```

```
1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→# Marker constants used by CLI and gateway to tag auto-reply messages.
    12→GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX = "autoreply-"
    13→CLI_INPUT_PREFIX = "[autoreply]"
    14→
    15→
    16→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    17→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    18→
    19→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    20→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    21→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    22→
    23→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    24→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    25→    """
    26→    # /autoreply off
    27→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    28→        return "off", None
    29→
    30→    # /autoreply max <N>
    31→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    32→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    33→        if len(parts) == 2:
    34→            try:
    35→                n = int(parts[1])
    36→                if n < 1:
    37→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    38→                return f"max:{n}", None
    39→            except ValueError:
    40→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    41→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    42→
    43→    # /autoreply (no args)
    44→    if not args:
    45→        return "status", None
    46→
    47→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    48→    literal = False
    49→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    50→    remaining_parts = []
    51→
    52→    parts = args.split()
    53→    i = 0
    54→    while i < len(parts):
    55→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    56→            literal = True
    57→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    58→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    59→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    60→            i += 1
    61→            try:
    62→                n = int(parts[i])
    63→                if n < 1:
    64→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    65→                max_turns = n
    66→            except ValueError:
    67→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    68→        else:
    69→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    70→        i += 1
    71→
    72→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    73→
    74→    if literal:
    75→        if not prompt:
    76→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    77→        return "enabled", {
    78→            "prompt": prompt,
    79→            "model": None,
    80→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    81→            "turn_count": 0,
    82→            "literal": True,
    83→        }
    84→
    85→    if not prompt:
    86→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    87→
    88→    return "enabled", {
    89→        "prompt": prompt,
    90→        "model": None,
    91→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    92→        "turn_count": 0,
    93→    }
    94→
    95→
    96→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    97→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    98→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    99→
   100→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
   101→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
   102→    """
   103→    recent = [
   104→        m for m in (history or [])
   105→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   106→    ][-20:]
   107→
   108→    system_prompt = (
   109→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   110→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   111→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   112→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   113→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   114→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   115→    )
   116→
   117→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   118→    for m in recent:
   119→        content = m["content"]
   120→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   121→        if isinstance(content, list):
   122→            content = "\n".join(
   123→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   124→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   125→            )
   126→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   127→    messages.append({
   128→        "role": "user",
   129→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   130→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   131→    })
   132→    return messages
   133→
   134→
   135→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   136→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   137→
   138→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   139→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   140→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   141→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   142→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   143→
   144→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   145→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   146→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   147→
   148→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   149→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   150→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   151→    """
   152→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   153→        return None, True
   154→
   155→    if config.get("literal"):
   156→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   157→        return config["prompt"], False
   158→
   159→    return None, False
   160→
   161→
   162→def prepare_llm_call(config: Dict[str, Any],
   163→                     history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   164→    """Build call_llm kwargs from config and history."""
   165→    messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
   166→    kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   167→        "task": "autoreply",
   168→        "messages": messages,
   169→        "temperature": 0.7,
   170→        "max_tokens": 1024,
   171→    }
   172→    if config.get("model"):
   173→        kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
   174→    return kwargs
   175→
   176→
   177→def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
   178→    """Extract reply text from LLM response. Returns text or None."""
   179→    if not response.choices:
   180→        return None
   181→    content = response.choices[0].message.content
   182→    if not content:
   183→        return None
   184→    config["turn_count"] += 1
   185→    return content.strip()
   186→
   187→
   188→def session_info(config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]) -> dict:
   189→    """Build autoreply status dict for the control API."""
   190→    return {
   191→        "autoreply": {
   192→            "enabled": config is not None,
   193→            "prompt": config.get("prompt", "") if config else None,
   194→            "max_turns": config.get("max_turns", 0) if config else None,
   195→            "turn_count": config.get("turn_count", 0) if config else None,
   196→        },
   197→    }
   198→
   199→
   200→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   201→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   202→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   203→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   204→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   205→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   206→        prompt_preview += "..."
   207→    return (
   208→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   209→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   210→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   211→    )
   212→
```

```
   138→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   139→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   140→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   141→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   142→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   143→
   144→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   145→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   146→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   147→
   148→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   149→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   150→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   151→    """
   152→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   153→        return None, True
   154→
   155→    if config.get("literal"):
   156→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   157→        return config["prompt"], False
   158→
   159→    return None, False
   160→
   161→
   162→def prepare_llm_call(config: Dict[str, Any],
   163→                     history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   164→    """Build call_llm kwargs from config and history."""
   165→    messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
   166→    kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   167→        "task": "autoreply",
   168→        "messages": messages,
   169→        "temperature": 0.7,
   170→        "max_tokens": 1024,
   171→    }
   172→    if config.get("model"):
   173→        kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
   174→    return kwargs
   175→
   176→
   177→def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
   178→    """Extract reply text from LLM response. Returns text or None."""
   179→    if not response.choices:
   180→        return None
   181→    content = response.choices[0].message.content
   182→    if not content:
   183→        return None
   184→    config["turn_count"] += 1
```

```
  1471→    def _start_control_api(self):
  1472→        """Start the control API in a background thread for external model switching."""
  1473→        try:
  1474→            import asyncio as _aio
  1475→            import threading
  1476→            from gateway.control_api import ControlAPI
  1477→
  1478→            shim = _CLIRunner(self.agent, self.session_id, self)
  1479→            api = ControlAPI(shim)
  1480→            ready = threading.Event()
  1481→
  1482→            def _run():
  1483→                try:
  1484→                    loop = _aio.new_event_loop()
  1485→                    _aio.set_event_loop(loop)
  1486→                    loop.run_until_complete(api.start())
  1487→                    ready.set()
  1488→                    loop.run_forever()
  1489→                except Exception as e:
  1490→                    logger.error("Control API thread crashed: %s", e)
  1491→                    ready.set()  # Unblock caller even on failure
  1492→
  1493→            t = threading.Thread(target=_run, daemon=True, name="control-api")
  1494→            t.start()
  1495→            ready.wait(timeout=5.0)
  1496→        except Exception as e:
  1497→            logger.debug("Control API failed to start: %s", e)
```

```
1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→# Marker constants used by CLI and gateway to tag auto-reply messages.
    12→GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX = "autoreply-"
    13→CLI_INPUT_PREFIX = "[autoreply]"
    14→
    15→
    16→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    17→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    18→
    19→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    20→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    21→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    22→
    23→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    24→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    25→    """
    26→    # /autoreply off
    27→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    28→        return "off", None
    29→
    30→    # /autoreply max <N>
    31→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    32→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    33→        if len(parts) == 2:
    34→            try:
    35→                n = int(parts[1])
    36→                if n < 1:
    37→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    38→                return f"max:{n}", None
    39→            except ValueError:
    40→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    41→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    42→
    43→    # /autoreply (no args)
    44→    if not args:
    45→        return "status", None
    46→
    47→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    48→    literal = False
    49→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    50→    remaining_parts = []
    51→
    52→    parts = args.split()
    53→    i = 0
    54→    while i < len(parts):
    55→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    56→            literal = True
    57→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    58→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    59→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    60→            i += 1
    61→            try:
    62→                n = int(parts[i])
    63→                if n < 1:
    64→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    65→                max_turns = n
    66→            except ValueError:
    67→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    68→        else:
    69→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    70→        i += 1
    71→
    72→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    73→
    74→    if literal:
    75→        if not prompt:
    76→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    77→        return "enabled", {
    78→            "prompt": prompt,
    79→            "model": None,
    80→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    81→            "turn_count": 0,
    82→            "literal": True,
    83→        }
    84→
    85→    if not prompt:
    86→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    87→
    88→    return "enabled", {
    89→        "prompt": prompt,
    90→        "model": None,
    91→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    92→        "turn_count": 0,
    93→    }
    94→
    95→
    96→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    97→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    98→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    99→
   100→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
   101→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
   102→    """
   103→    recent = [
   104→        m for m in (history or [])
   105→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   106→    ][-20:]
   107→
   108→    system_prompt = (
   109→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   110→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   111→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   112→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   113→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   114→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   115→    )
   116→
   117→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   118→    for m in recent:
   119→        content = m["content"]
   120→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   121→        if isinstance(content, list):
   122→            content = "\n".join(
   123→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   124→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   125→            )
   126→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   127→    messages.append({
   128→        "role": "user",
   129→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   130→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   131→    })
   132→    return messages
   133→
   134→
   135→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   136→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   137→
   138→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   139→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   140→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   141→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   142→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   143→
   144→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   145→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   146→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   147→
   148→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   149→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   150→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   151→    """
   152→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   153→        return None, True
   154→
   155→    if config.get("literal"):
   156→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   157→        return config["prompt"], False
   158→
   159→    return None, False
   160→
   161→
   162→def prepare_llm_call(config: Dict[str, Any],
   163→                     history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   164→    """Build call_llm kwargs from config and history."""
   165→    messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
   166→    kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   167→        "task": "autoreply",
   168→        "messages": messages,
   169→        "temperature": 0.7,
   170→        "max_tokens": 1024,
   171→    }
   172→    if config.get("model"):
   173→        kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
   174→    return kwargs
   175→
   176→
   177→def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
   178→    """Extract reply text from LLM response. Returns text or None."""
   179→    if not response.choices:
   180→        return None
   181→    content = response.choices[0].message.content
   182→    if not content:
   183→        return None
   184→    config["turn_count"] += 1
   185→    return content.strip()
   186→
   187→
   188→def session_info(config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]) -> dict:
   189→    """Build autoreply status dict for the control API."""
   190→    return {
   191→        "autoreply": {
   192→            "enabled": config is not None,
   193→            "prompt": config.get("prompt", "") if config else None,
   194→            "max_turns": config.get("max_turns", 0) if config else None,
   195→            "turn_count": config.get("turn_count", 0) if config else None,
   196→        },
   197→    }
   198→
   199→
   200→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   201→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   202→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   203→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   204→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   205→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   206→        prompt_preview += "..."
   207→    return (
   208→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   209→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   210→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   211→    )
   212→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.
    30→AVAILABLE_COMMANDS = [
    31→    {"command": "/reset", "description": "Reset conversation history"},
    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <prompt>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop (LLM generates replies from your prompt)"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply --literal <message>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop (sends exact message each turn)"},
    41→    {"command": "/autoreply --forever <prompt>", "description": "Auto-reply with no turn limit (default: 20)"},
    42→    {"command": "/autoreply --max N <prompt>", "description": "Auto-reply with custom turn limit"},
    43→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    44→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    45→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    46→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    47→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    48→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    49→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    50→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    51→]

    53→
    54→def _get_port() -> int:
    55→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    56→
    57→
    58→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    59→    return web.Response(
    60→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    61→        content_type="application/json",
    62→        status=status,
    63→    )
    64→
    65→
    66→REQUIRED_HEADER = "X-Hermes-Control"
    67→
    68→
    69→class ControlAPI:
    70→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    71→
    72→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    73→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    74→        self.app = web.Application(middlewares=[self._require_header])
    75→        self._setup_routes()
    76→        self._site = None
    77→
    78→    @web.middleware
    79→    async def _require_header(self, request: web.Request, handler):
    80→        """Reject requests missing the X-Hermes-Control header.
    81→
    82→        This blocks browser-based CSRF attacks: any non-standard header
    83→        forces a CORS preflight, which fails because we don't serve
    84→        Access-Control-Allow-* headers.  Non-browser callers just add
    85→        the header.
    86→        """
    87→        if request.headers.get(REQUIRED_HEADER) != "1":
    88→            return _json_response(
    89→                {"error": f"Missing required header: {REQUIRED_HEADER}: 1"},
    90→                status=403,
    91→            )
    92→        return await handler(request)
    93→
    94→    def _setup_routes(self):
    95→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    96→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    97→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    98→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    99→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
   100→
   101→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   102→
   103→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
   104→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
   105→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
   106→        if key == "_any":
   107→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
   108→                return key, None
   109→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
   110→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
   111→
   112→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   113→
   114→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   115→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
   116→
   117→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   118→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
   119→        sessions = []
   120→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
   121→            entry = {
   122→                "session_key": key,
   123→                "status": "running",
   124→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   125→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   126→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   127→            }
   128→            sessions.append(entry)
   129→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})
   130→

   132→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   133→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   134→        if not agent:
   135→            return _json_response(
   136→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   137→                status=404,
   138→            )
   139→        return _json_response({
   140→            "session_key": key,
   141→            "status": "running",
   142→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   143→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   144→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   145→        })
   146→
   147→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   148→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   149→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   150→
   151→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   152→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   153→
   154→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   155→
   156→        Modes:
   157→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   158→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   159→        """
   160→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   161→
   162→        try:
   163→            body = await request.json()
   164→        except Exception:
   165→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   166→
   167→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   168→        if not text:
   169→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   170→
   171→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   172→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   173→            return _json_response(
   174→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   175→                status=400,
   176→            )
   177→
   178→        logger.info(
   179→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   180→            key, mode, text[:80],
   181→        )
   182→
   183→        try:
   184→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   185→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   186→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   187→                await result
   188→        except LookupError as e:
   189→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   190→
   191→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   192→
   193→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   194→
   195→    async def start(self):
   196→        port = _get_port()
   197→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   198→        await app_runner.setup()
   199→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   200→        try:
   201→            await self._site.start()
   202→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   203→            self._write_port_file(port)
   204→        except OSError as e:
   205→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   206→
   207→    async def stop(self):
   208→        if self._site:
   209→            await self._site.stop()
   210→            self._remove_port_file()
   211→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   212→
   213→    @staticmethod
   214→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   215→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   216→        try:
   217→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   218→                f.write(str(port))
   219→        except OSError:
   220→            pass
   221→
   222→    @staticmethod
   223→    def _remove_port_file():
   224→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   225→        try:
   226→            os.remove(path)
   227→        except OSError:
   228→            pass
```

```
Preview (first 2KB):
     1→"""Shared auxiliary OpenAI client for cheap/fast side tasks.
     2→
     3→Provides a single resolution chain so every consumer (context compression,
     4→session search, web extraction, vision analysis, browser vision) picks up
     5→the best available backend without duplicating fallback logic.
     6→
     7→Resolution order for text tasks (auto mode):
     8→  1. OpenRouter  (OPENROUTER_API_KEY)
     9→  2. Nous Portal (~/.hermes/auth.json active provider)
    10→  3. Custom endpoint (OPENAI_BASE_URL + OPENAI_API_KEY)
    11→  4. Codex OAuth (Responses API via chatgpt.com with gpt-5.3-codex,
    12→     wrapped to look like a chat.completions client)
    13→  5. Direct API-key providers (z.ai/GLM, Kimi/Moonshot, MiniMax, MiniMax-CN)
    14→     — checked via PROVIDER_REGISTRY entries with auth_type='api_key'
    15→  6. None
    16→
    17→Resolution order for vision/multimodal tasks (auto mode):
    18→  1. OpenRouter
    19→  2. Nous Portal
    20→  3. Codex OAuth (gpt-5.3-codex supports vision via Responses API)
    21→  4. Custom endpoint (for local vision models: Qwen-VL, LLaVA, Pixtral, etc.)
    22→  5. None  (API-key providers like z.ai/Kimi/MiniMax are skipped —
    23→     they may not support multimodal)
    24→
    25→Per-task provider overrides (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER,
    26→CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_PROVIDER) can force a specific provider for each task:
    27→"openrouter", "nous", "codex", or "main" (= steps 3-5).
    28→Default "auto" follows the chains above.
    29→
    30→Per-task model overrides (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_MODEL,
    31→AUXILIARY_WEB_EXTRACT_MODEL) let callers use a different model slug
    32→than the provider's default.
    33→"""

    36→import logging
    37→import os

    39→from types import SimpleNamespace
```

```
     1→"""Shared auxiliary OpenAI client for cheap/fast side tasks.
     2→
     3→Provides a single resolution chain so every consumer (context compression,
     4→session search, web extraction, vision analysis, browser vision) picks up
     5→the best available backend without duplicating fallback logic.
     6→
     7→Resolution order for text tasks (auto mode):
     8→  1. OpenRouter  (OPENROUTER_API_KEY)
     9→  2. Nous Portal (~/.hermes/auth.json active provider)
    10→  3. Custom endpoint (OPENAI_BASE_URL + OPENAI_API_KEY)
    11→  4. Codex OAuth (Responses API via chatgpt.com with gpt-5.3-codex,
    12→     wrapped to look like a chat.completions client)
    13→  5. Direct API-key providers (z.ai/GLM, Kimi/Moonshot, MiniMax, MiniMax-CN)
    14→     — checked via PROVIDER_REGISTRY entries with auth_type='api_key'
    15→  6. None
    16→
    17→Resolution order for vision/multimodal tasks (auto mode):
    18→  1. OpenRouter
    19→  2. Nous Portal
    20→  3. Codex OAuth (gpt-5.3-codex supports vision via Responses API)
    21→  4. Custom endpoint (for local vision models: Qwen-VL, LLaVA, Pixtral, etc.)
    22→  5. None  (API-key providers like z.ai/Kimi/MiniMax are skipped —
    23→     they may not support multimodal)
    24→
    25→Per-task provider overrides (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER,
    26→CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_PROVIDER) can force a specific provider for each task:
    27→"openrouter", "nous", "codex", or "main" (= steps 3-5).
    28→Default "auto" follows the chains above.
    29→
    30→Per-task model overrides (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_MODEL,
    31→AUXILIARY_WEB_EXTRACT_MODEL) let callers use a different model slug
    32→than the provider's default.
    33→"""
```

```
def _generate_autoreply_text(self) -> Optional[str]:
        """Generate the next auto-reply text, or None if done."""
        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, prepare_llm_call, extract_reply

        config = self._autoreply_config
        if not config:
            return None

        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
        if cap_reached:
            self._autoreply_config = None
            _cprint(f"\n{_DIM}[Auto-reply limit reached. Use /autoreply to re-enable.]{_RST}")
            return None
        if text:
            return text

        # LLM-generated: build prompt from conversation history and call sync LLM
        from agent.auxiliary_client import call_llm

        call_kwargs = prepare_llm_call(config, self.conversation_history or [])
        response = call_llm(**call_kwargs)
--

                            if reply:

                                _cprint(f"\n{_DIM}[Auto-reply {turn}/{max_t}]{_RST}")
```

```
    async def _generate_autoreply(self, session_key: str, session_id: str):
        """Generate an auto-reply using the auxiliary LLM client.

        Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).  reply_text is None when
        auto-reply is inactive or the turn cap was just hit.
        """
        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, prepare_llm_call, extract_reply

        config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
        if not config:
            return None, False

        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
        if cap_reached:
            del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
            return None, True
        if text:
            return text, False

        # LLM-generated: build prompt from transcript and call async LLM
        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)
        call_kwargs = prepare_llm_call(config, history)

        from agent.auxiliary_client import async_call_llm
        response = await async_call_llm(**call_kwargs)
        reply_text = extract_reply(config, response)
        return reply_text, False

    async def _process_autoreply(self, source, session_key: str, session_id: str):
        """Fire-and-forget task: generate an auto-reply and inject it via the adapter."""
```

```
if config.get("model"):
        kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
    return kwargs


def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
    """Extract reply text from LLM response. Returns text or None."""
    if not response.choices:
        return None
    content = response.choices[0].message.content
    if not content:
        return None
    config["turn_count"] += 1
    return content.strip()
```

Perfect. Now I have enough context. Let me create a detailed analysis document:

## Detailed Analysis: `agent/autoreply.py` API Design Quality

I've reviewed the autoreply module thoroughly, including its usage in `cli.py`, `gateway/run.py`, and the test suite. Here are the specific, actionable issues:

---

### 1. **CONFIG DICT MUTATION (CRITICAL BUG)**

**Issue:** `extract_reply()` and `check_and_advance()` directly mutate the config dict passed in.

**Lines affected:**
- Line 156: `config["turn_count"] += 1` (in `check_and_advance`)
- Line 184: `config["turn_count"] += 1` (in `extract_reply`)

**Why it's risky:** The config dict is passed around and stored at the caller level (`self._autoreply_config` in CLI, `self._autoreply_configs[key]` in gateway). While the tests work because they expect this behavior, mutating caller-owned state is fragile:
- If a caller accidentally passes the same config to multiple functions in sequence, state tracking becomes confusing
- Immutability would make the logic clearer: these functions should compute new state, not modify mutable objects
- If future refactoring threads these functions differently, bugs become likely

**Recommendation:** Return updated config or new turn_count explicitly, don't mutate. If mutation is intentional by design, document it prominently in the docstrings.

---

### 2. **MISSING NONE CHECK IN `format_status()` (CRASH BUG)**

**Lines 200-211:**
```python
def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]  # ← CRASH if config is None
    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
        prompt_preview += "..."
```

**Issue:** The function signature expects `Dict[str, Any]`, but the docstring for `session_info()` (line 188) shows that config can be None. The caller at `cli.py:2970` does check before calling:
```python
if self._autoreply_config:
    _cprint("🔄 " + format_status(self._autoreply_config))
```

However, the type signature doesn't reflect the None-checking requirement. If a future caller forgets the None check, **KeyError** on line 204.

**Recommendation:** Change signature to `Optional[Dict[str, Any]]` and add guard:
```python
def format_status(config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]) -> str:
    if not config:
        return "Auto-reply is not active."
    # ... rest of function
```

---

### 3. **INCONSISTENT DOCSTRING IN `check_and_advance()` (CONFUSING API CONTRACT)**

**Lines 135-151:**

The docstring is convoluted:
```python
"""Check turn cap and handle literal mode.

Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
- (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
- (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
- (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
- None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM

For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).

So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
"""
```

**Issue:** The contract is buried in confusing prose. The function is **never actually called with None** (see `cli.py:2989-2991` and `gateway/run.py:2047-2049`), so the first bullet point is misleading. The actual callers all check `if not config:` before calling.

**Recommendation:** Simplify docstring to document what actually happens:
```python
"""Check turn cap and advance literal mode if applicable.

Args:
    config: Non-None autoreply config dict

Returns (reply_text, cap_reached):
    - (None, True): max_turns exceeded, caller should disable autoreply
    - (text, False): literal mode—return this text and increment turn_count
    - (None, False): LLM mode—caller should proceed to LLM call
"""
```

---

### 4. **MISSING NONE CHECKS IN `prepare_llm_call()` AND `extract_reply()` (BRITTLE TYPE CONTRACTS)**

**Lines 162-175 and 177-185:**

Both functions have signatures like:
```python
def prepare_llm_call(config: Dict[str, Any], history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> ...
def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
```

But **neither function validates that config is not None**. The callers do check (see `gateway/run.py:2047-2049`), but:
1. If a future caller skips the None check, the function will crash with a cryptic AttributeError
2. The type signature should reflect the contract

**Why it matters:** `session_info()` takes `Optional[Dict[str, Any]]`, establishing that None is a valid state in the module. But other functions silently assume non-None without documenting this.

**Recommendation:** Either:
- Change signatures to `Optional[Dict[str, Any]]` and add guard clauses, OR
- Document prominently in module docstring: "All config parameters must be non-None; callers must validate before calling these functions"

---

### 5. **RESPONSE PARAMETER TYPE IS NOT ANNOTATED IN `extract_reply()` (MISSING TYPE INFO)**

**Line 177:**
```python
def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
```

The `response` parameter has no type annotation. Based on usage at line 181:
```python
content = response.choices[0].message.content
```

This expects an object compatible with the OpenRouter/Anthropic API response shape. 

**Recommendation:** Import and use the actual response type:
```python
from anthropic.types.completion import Completion  # or appropriate type

def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response: Completion) -> Optional[str]:
```

Or document what shape response must have.

---

### 6. **EDGE CASE: `build_autoreply_messages()` SILENTLY DROPS MALFORMED CONTENT (LINE 121-125)**

**Lines 121-125:**
```python
if isinstance(content, list):
    content = "\n".join(
        p.get("text", "") for p in content
        if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
    )
```

**Issue:** If content is a list but contains no text-type items, this silently produces an empty string. The message is then appended with empty content (line 126). This is probably fine for robustness, but it's a silent failure mode.

**Recommendation:** Log a debug message or add a comment explaining this is intentional for malformed content.

---

### 7. **INCONSISTENT FIELD PRESENCE IN CONFIG DICTS**

**Lines 77-93 in `parse_autoreply_args()`:**

The function creates config dicts with different field sets:
- Literal mode (lines 77-83): includes `"literal": True`
- LLM mode (lines 88-93): omits `"literal"` key entirely

**Issue:** Later code uses `config.get("literal")` (lines 155, 202, 203), which safely handles the missing key. However, this means:
- Callers can't rely on `"literal"` being present
- Tests work around this by explicitly setting it (see `test_cli_autoreply.py:134`)

**Recommendation:** Always include `"literal": False` in the LLM-mode dict (line 93) for consistency:
```python
return "enabled", {
    "prompt": prompt,
    "model": None,
    "max_turns": max_turns,
    "turn_count": 0,
    "literal": False,  # ← Add this for consistency
}
```

---

### 8. **PARSE_AUTOREPLY_ARGS() HAS REDUNDANT VALIDATION (LINE 31-41)**

**Lines 31-41:**
```python
if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    parts = args.split(None, 1)
    if len(parts) == 2:
        # ... validate and parse
    return "error:..." # ← This branch never executes; parts is always ≥1
    return "error:..."
```

**Issue:** The condition `args.lower().startswith("max ")` already guarantees `len(parts) == 2` after split (since there's a space). The fallthrough error at line 41 is dead code. The condition `args.lower() == "max"` on line 31 should be handled separately.

**Recommendation:** Refactor for clarity:
```python
if args.lower() == "max" or args.lower().startswith("max "):
    parts = args.split(None, 1)
    if len(parts) != 2:
        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    try:
        n = int(parts[1])
        if n < 1:
            return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
        return f"max:{n}", None
    except ValueError:
        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
```

---

### 9. **NO VALIDATION OF `max_turns` AGAINST TURN_COUNT AT CALL TIME**

**Observation:** There's a logical gap. In `check_and_advance()` line 152:
```python
if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
    return None, True
```

The check is `turn_count >= max_turns`, which is correct. However, there's no runtime assertion that `max_turns` wasn't modified to be invalid (e.g., negative) between config creation and this check. The CLI does allow `/autoreply max N` to mutate the config (cli.py:2962), so a user could theoretically set max to 0 while a turn is in progress.

**Recommendation:** Add bounds checking when max_turns is modified (in caller code, not autoreply.py), or document this edge case.

---

### 10. **MODULE-LEVEL COHESION: ARE ALL FUNCTIONS HERE?**

The module groups:
- Config parsing (`parse_autoreply_args`)
- Message building (`build_autoreply_messages`)
- LLM call preparation (`prepare_llm_call`)
- Response extraction (`extract_reply`)
- State management (`check_and_advance`)
- Status formatting (`format_status`, `session_info`)
- Constants (`_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS`, `GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX`, `CLI_INPUT_PREFIX`)

**Assessment:** Yes, this is cohesive—all functions relate to the autoreply lifecycle. No functions belong elsewhere.

---

## Summary Table

| Issue | Severity | Line(s) | Type | Fix |
|-------|----------|---------|------|-----|
| Config dict mutation in `extract_reply`, `check_and_advance` | High | 156, 184 | Design | Return new state instead of mutating; or document explicitly |
| Missing None check in `format_status()` | High | 200-211 | Crash bug | Add `Optional[Dict]` type and None guard |
| Confusing `check_and_advance()` docstring | Medium | 135-151 | Docs | Simplify to reflect actual contract |
| No None validation in LLM functions | Medium | 162-185 | Type safety | Add `Optional[Dict]` or document requirement |
| `response` parameter untyped in `extract_reply()` | Low | 177 | Type coverage | Add proper type annotation |
| `build_autoreply_messages()` silent empty-content drop | Low | 121-125 | Robustness | Add debug log or comment |
| Inconsistent field presence in config (missing `"literal"`) | Low | 93 | Consistency | Always set `"literal"` key |
| Redundant validation in `parse_autoreply_args()` | Low | 31-41 | Code quality | Refactor for clarity |
| No max_turns mutation bounds checking | Low | N/A | Edge case | Document in caller code |

The **critical issues** are #1 (mutation) and #2 (crash in format_status). These should be fixed before the API is considered stable.

```
  1444→        from agent.autoreply import GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX
  1445→        is_autoreply = (event.message_id or "").startswith(GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX)
  1446→        if session_key in self._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
  1447→            self._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
  1448→
  1449→        try:
  1450→            # Emit agent:start hook
  1451→            hook_ctx = {
  1452→                "platform": source.platform.value if source.platform else "",
  1453→                "user_id": source.user_id,
  1454→                "session_id": session_entry.session_id,
  1455→                "message": message_text[:500],
  1456→            }
```

```
   282→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   283→    async def test_generates_reply_and_increments_counter(self):
   284→        runner = _make_runner()
   285→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   286→            "prompt": "Ask follow-up questions",
   287→            "model": None,
   288→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS,
   289→            "turn_count": 2,
   290→        }
   291→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   292→            {"role": "user", "content": "Tell me about Python"},
   293→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "Python is a programming language."},
   294→        ]
   295→
   296→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   297→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   298→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "What about its type system?"
   299→
   300→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   301→            text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   302→
   303→        assert text == "What about its type system?"
   304→        assert cap is False
   305→        assert runner._autoreply_configs["key"]["turn_count"] == 3
   306→
   307→        # Verify the LLM was called with task="autoreply"
   308→        call_kwargs = mock_llm.call_args[1]
   309→        assert call_kwargs["task"] == "autoreply"
   310→        assert call_kwargs["temperature"] == 0.7
   311→        # System prompt should contain the user's instructions
   312→        system_msg = call_kwargs["messages"][0]
   313→        assert system_msg["role"] == "system"
   314→        assert "Ask follow-up questions" in system_msg["content"]
   315→
   316→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   317→    async def test_flattens_list_content(self):
   318→        """Messages with list-type content are flattened to text."""
   319→        runner = _make_runner()
   320→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   321→            "prompt": "Continue", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 0,
   322→        }
   323→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   324→            {"role": "user", "content": [
   325→                {"type": "text", "text": "Look at this image"},
   326→                {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": "http://example.com/img.png"}},
   327→            ]},
   328→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "I see the image."},
   329→        ]
   330→
   331→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   332→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   333→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "What else can you tell me?"
   334→
   335→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   336→            await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   337→
   338→        # The user message should have been flattened to text only
   339→        messages = mock_llm.call_args[1]["messages"]
   340→        user_msg = [m for m in messages if m["role"] == "user" and "Look at" in m.get("content", "")]
   341→        assert len(user_msg) == 1
   342→        assert user_msg[0]["content"] == "Look at this image"
   343→
```
