1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import logging
     8→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     9→
    10→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    11→
    12→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    13→
    14→# Marker constants used by CLI and gateway to tag auto-reply messages.
    15→GATEWAY_MSG_PREFIX = "autoreply-"
    16→CLI_INPUT_PREFIX = "[autoreply]"
    17→
    18→
    19→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    20→    """Parse /autoreply arguments into (action, config_or_none).
    21→
    22→    Actions: "off", "max:N", "error:msg", "status", "enabled".
    23→    Used internally by handle_command(); prefer that for full dispatch.
    24→    """
    25→    # /autoreply off
    26→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    27→        return "off", None
    28→
    29→    # /autoreply max <N>
    30→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    31→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    32→        if len(parts) == 2:
    33→            try:
    34→                n = int(parts[1])
    35→                if n < 1:
    36→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    37→                return f"max:{n}", None
    38→            except ValueError:
    39→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    40→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    41→
    42→    # /autoreply (no args)
    43→    if not args:
    44→        return "status", None
    45→
    46→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    47→    literal = False
    48→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    49→    remaining_parts = []
    50→
    51→    parts = args.split()
    52→    i = 0
    53→    while i < len(parts):
    54→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    55→            literal = True
    56→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    57→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    58→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    59→            i += 1
    60→            try:
    61→                n = int(parts[i])
    62→                if n < 1:
    63→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    64→                max_turns = n
    65→            except ValueError:
    66→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    67→        else:
    68→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    69→        i += 1
    70→
    71→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    72→
    73→    if literal:
    74→        if not prompt:
    75→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    76→        return "enabled", {
    77→            "prompt": prompt,
    78→            "model": None,
    79→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    80→            "turn_count": 0,
    81→            "literal": True,
    82→        }
    83→
    84→    if not prompt:
    85→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    86→
    87→    return "enabled", {
    88→        "prompt": prompt,
    89→        "model": None,
    90→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    91→        "turn_count": 0,
    92→    }
    93→
    94→
    95→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    96→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    97→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    98→
    99→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
   100→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
   101→    """
   102→    recent = [
   103→        m for m in (history or [])
   104→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   105→    ][-20:]
   106→
   107→    system_prompt = (
   108→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   109→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   110→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   111→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   112→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   113→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   114→    )
   115→
   116→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   117→    for m in recent:
   118→        content = m["content"]
   119→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   120→        if isinstance(content, list):
   121→            content = "\n".join(
   122→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   123→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   124→            )
   125→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   126→    messages.append({
   127→        "role": "user",
   128→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   129→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   130→    })
   131→    return messages
   132→
   133→
   134→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   135→    """Check turn cap and return literal text if applicable.
   136→
   137→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached):
   138→    - (None, True)  — cap hit, caller should remove config
   139→    - (text, False)  — literal mode reply
   140→    - (None, False) — caller should proceed to LLM call
   141→    """
   142→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   143→        return None, True
   144→
   145→    if config.get("literal"):
   146→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   147→        return config["prompt"], False
   148→
   149→    return None, False
   150→
   151→
   152→def prepare_llm_call(config: Dict[str, Any],
   153→                     history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   154→    """Build call_llm kwargs from config and history."""
   155→    messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
   156→    kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   157→        "task": "autoreply",
   158→        "messages": messages,
   159→        "temperature": 0.7,
   160→        "max_tokens": 1024,
   161→    }
   162→    if config.get("model"):
   163→        kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
   164→    return kwargs
   165→
   166→
   167→def extract_reply(config: Dict[str, Any], response) -> Optional[str]:
   168→    """Extract reply text from LLM response, or None on empty/missing content.
   169→
   170→    Increments config["turn_count"] only on success.  Returns None (without
   171→    incrementing) when the LLM returns no choices or empty content, so the
   172→    caller can decide how to handle the gap.
   173→    """
   174→    if not response.choices:
   175→        logger.warning("[AutoReply] LLM returned no choices")
   176→        return None
   177→    content = response.choices[0].message.content
   178→    if not content:
   179→        logger.warning("[AutoReply] LLM returned empty content")
   180→        return None
   181→    config["turn_count"] += 1
   182→    return content.strip()
   183→
   184→
   185→def session_info(config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]) -> dict:
   186→    """Build autoreply status dict for the control API."""
   187→    return {
   188→        "autoreply": {
   189→            "enabled": config is not None,
   190→            "prompt": config.get("prompt", "") if config else None,
   191→            "max_turns": config.get("max_turns", 0) if config else None,
   192→            "turn_count": config.get("turn_count", 0) if config else None,
   193→        },
   194→    }
   195→
   196→
   197→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   198→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   199→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   200→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   201→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   202→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   203→        prompt_preview += "..."
   204→    return (
   205→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   206→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   207→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   208→    )
   209→
   210→
   211→def handle_command(
   212→    args: str, current_config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
   213→) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
   214→    """Handle /autoreply command — full dispatch.
   215→
   216→    Returns (display_text, new_config).
   217→    - new_config is None when autoreply should be disabled/inactive.
   218→    - new_config is the (possibly mutated) config when active.
   219→    - display_text is ready to show to the user.
   220→    """
   221→    action, new_config = parse_autoreply_args(args)
   222→
   223→    if action == "off":
   224→        if current_config:
   225→            return "🔇 Auto-reply disabled.", None
   226→        return "Auto-reply is not active.", None
   227→
   228→    if action.startswith("max:"):
   229→        n = int(action.split(":")[1])
   230→        if current_config:
   231→            current_config["max_turns"] = n
   232→            return f"🔄 Auto-reply max turns set to {n}.", current_config
   233→        return "Auto-reply is not active. Use /autoreply <instructions> first.", None
   234→
   235→    if action.startswith("error:"):
   236→        return action[6:], current_config
   237→
   238→    if action == "status":
   239→        if current_config:
   240→            return "🔄 " + format_status(current_config), current_config
   241→        return "Auto-reply is not active. Use /autoreply <instructions> to enable.", None
   242→
   243→    # action == "enabled"
   244→    mode = "literal mode " if new_config.get("literal") else ""
   245→    label = "Message" if new_config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   246→    max_t = new_config["max_turns"]
   247→    prompt_preview = new_config["prompt"][:100]
   248→    if len(new_config["prompt"]) > 100:
   249→        prompt_preview += "..."
   250→    text = (
   251→        f"🔄 Auto-reply enabled — {mode}({'forever' if max_t == 0 else f'max {max_t} turns'}).\n"
   252→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   253→        f"  Send a message to start the loop. Use /autoreply off to stop."
   254→    )
   255→    return text, new_config
   256→
```

1→"""Unit tests for agent/autoreply.py — shared autoreply engine.
     2→
     3→Tests every public function in isolation, with focus on edge cases
     4→that the integration tests (test_cli_autoreply.py, test_autoreply_command.py)
     5→don't cover.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from unittest.mock import MagicMock

    43→class TestParseAutoreplyArgs:
    44→    def test_empty_string_returns_status(self):

    48→        assert parse_autoreply_args("off") == ("off", None)

    52→            action, cfg = parse_autoreply_args(word)

    55→    def test_simple_instructions(self):

    57→        assert action == "enabled"

   200→        """Non-dict elements in list content are silently skipped."""
   201→        history = [
   202→            {"role": "user", "content": ["raw string", {"type": "text", "text": "ok"}]},
   203→        ]
   204→        msgs = build_autoreply_messages(self._config(), history)
   205→        assert msgs[1]["content"] == "ok"

   211→class TestCheckAndAdvance:
   212→    def test_cap_reached(self):
   213→        config = {"max_turns": 5, "turn_count": 5}
   214→        text, cap = check_and_advance(config)
   215→        assert text is None
   216→        assert cap is True

   218→    def test_cap_exceeded(self):

   222→        assert cap is True

   224→    def test_under_cap_llm_mode(self):
   225→        config = {"max_turns": 5, "turn_count": 2}
   226→        text, cap = check_and_advance(config)
   227→        assert text is None
   228→        assert cap is False
   229→
   230→    def test_unlimited_mode(self):
   231→        """max_turns=0 means unlimited — should never trigger cap."""
   232→        config = {"max_turns": 0, "turn_count": 1000}
   233→        text, cap = check_and_advance(config)
   234→        assert text is None
   235→        assert cap is False

   237→    def test_literal_mode_returns_prompt(self):
   238→        config = {"max_turns": 20, "turn_count": 0, "literal": True, "prompt": "continue"}
   239→        text, cap = check_and_advance(config)
   240→        assert text == "continue"
   241→        assert cap is False
   242→        assert config["turn_count"] == 1
   243→
   244→    def test_literal_mode_cap_takes_precedence(self):
   245→        """Cap check happens before literal check."""
   246→        config = {"max_turns": 3, "turn_count": 3, "literal": True, "prompt": "continue"}
   247→        text, cap = check_and_advance(config)
   248→        assert text is None
   249→        assert cap is True
   250→        # turn_count should NOT have been incremented
   251→        assert config["turn_count"] == 3
   252→
   253→    def test_boundary_turn_count_equals_max_minus_one(self):
   254→        """Last allowed turn."""

   256→        text, cap = check_and_advance(config)
   257→        assert text == "go"
   258→        assert config["turn_count"] == 5

   352→
   353→class TestSessionInfo:
   354→    def test_none_config(self):
   355→        info = session_info(None)
   356→        assert info["autoreply"]["enabled"] is False
   357→        assert info["autoreply"]["prompt"] is None
   358→        assert info["autoreply"]["max_turns"] is None
   359→        assert info["autoreply"]["turn_count"] is None
   360→
   361→    def test_with_config(self):
   362→        cfg = {"prompt": "test", "max_turns": 10, "turn_count": 3}
   363→        info = session_info(cfg)
   364→        assert info["autoreply"]["enabled"] is True
   365→        assert info["autoreply"]["prompt"] == "test"
   366→        assert info["autoreply"]["max_turns"] == 10
   367→        assert info["autoreply"]["turn_count"] == 3
   368→
   369→    def test_empty_dict_treated_as_disabled(self):
   370→        """An empty dict is falsy in Python, so it's treated as disabled."""
   371→        info = session_info({})
   372→        # {} is falsy → enabled is True (config is not None) but fields use None
   373→        # because `if config` is False for empty dict
   374→        assert info["autoreply"]["enabled"] is True
   375→        assert info["autoreply"]["prompt"] is None
   376→
   377→    def test_config_with_minimal_keys(self):
   378→        """Config with just the required keys works correctly."""
   379→        cfg = {"prompt": "test", "max_turns": 20, "turn_count": 0}
   380→        info = session_info(cfg)
   381→        assert info["autoreply"]["enabled"] is True
   382→        assert info["autoreply"]["prompt"] == "test"
   383→        assert info["autoreply"]["max_turns"] == 20
   384→        assert info["autoreply"]["turn_count"] == 0
   385→
   386→    def test_cli_and_gateway_parity(self):
   387→        """Both CLI and gateway should produce identical output for the same config."""
   388→        cfg = {"prompt": "test", "max_turns": 20, "turn_count": 5}
   389→        # Both paths now call session_info() directly
   390→        result = session_info(cfg)
   391→        assert result == {
   392→            "autoreply": {
   393→                "enabled": True,
   394→                "prompt": "test",
   395→                "max_turns": 20,
   396→                "turn_count": 5,
   397→            },
   398→        }

   404→class TestFormatStatus:

   407→        result = format_status(cfg)
   408→        assert "LLM-generated" in result

   411→        assert "Ask questions" in result
   412→
   413→    def test_literal_mode(self):

   415→        result = format_status(cfg)

   418→
   419→    def test_unlimited_mode(self):
   420→        cfg = {"prompt": "test", "max_turns": 0, "turn_count": 5}
   421→        result = format_status(cfg)
   422→        assert "5/∞" in result
   423→
   424→    def test_long_prompt_truncated(self):
   425→        cfg = {"prompt": "A" * 200, "max_turns": 20, "turn_count": 0}
   426→        result = format_status(cfg)
   427→        assert "..." in result
   428→        assert "A" * 200 not in result
   429→        assert "A" * 100 in result

   296→class TestCLIInputPrefix:

   299→    def test_prefix_value(self):

   301→
   302→    def test_prefix_stripping(self):
   303→        """Simulate the prefix-stripping logic from the CLI input loop."""
   304→        user_input = f"{CLI_INPUT_PREFIX}What about types?"
   305→        if user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   306→            user_input = user_input[len(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):]

   309→    def test_real_message_does_not_strip(self):
   310→        user_input = "What about types?"
   311→        if user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   312→            user_input = user_input[len(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):]
   313→        assert user_input == "What about types?"
   314→
   315→    def test_turn_counter_reset_on_real_message(self):
   316→        """Real messages reset turn counter; prefixed messages don't."""
   317→        config = {"turn_count": 5}
   318→
   319→        # Real message resets
   320→        user_input = "hello"
   321→        if not user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   322→            config["turn_count"] = 0
   323→        assert config["turn_count"] == 0
   324→
   325→        # Autoreply message does not reset
   326→        config["turn_count"] = 5
   327→        user_input = f"{CLI_INPUT_PREFIX}generated reply"
   328→        if not user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
   329→            config["turn_count"] = 0
   330→        assert config["turn_count"] == 5
   331→

   579→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   580→# Turn counter reset & auto-reply label in _handle_message
   581→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   582→
   583→
   584→class TestAutoreplyInHandleMessage:
   585→    """Tests for auto-reply wiring in _handle_message.
   586→
   587→    These test the auto-reply logic directly rather than going through the
   588→    full _handle_message pipeline, which requires too much setup. The
   589→    individual methods (_generate_autoreply, _process_autoreply) are
   590→    thoroughly tested above; here we verify the wiring code itself.
   591→    """
   592→
   593→    def test_autoreply_message_detected_by_message_id(self):
   594→        """Messages with autoreply- prefix in message_id are detected."""
   595→        event = _make_event(text="test", message_id="autoreply-123.456")
   596→        assert (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   597→
   598→        event2 = _make_event(text="test", message_id="regular-123")
   599→        assert not (event2.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   600→
   601→    def test_turn_counter_reset_logic(self):
   602→        """Real messages reset counter; autoreply messages don't."""
   603→        runner = _make_runner()
   604→        session_key = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
   605→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   606→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 7,
   607→        }
   608→
   609→        # Real message — should reset
   610→        is_autoreply = ("regular-msg" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   611→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   612→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   613→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 0
   614→
   615→        # Restore and test autoreply message — should NOT reset
   616→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 7
   617→        is_autoreply = ("autoreply-123.456" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   618→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   619→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   620→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 7
   621→
   622→    def test_response_labeling_logic(self):
   623→        """Auto-reply responses get labeled with turn count."""
   624→        runner = _make_runner()
   625→        session_key = "test_key"
   626→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   627→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 3,
   628→        }
   629→
   630→        # Simulate the labeling code from _handle_message
   631→        is_autoreply = True
   632→        response = "Agent response here"
   633→        event_text = "Generated question"
   634→
   635→        if is_autoreply and response:
   636→            ar_config = runner._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
   637→            if ar_config:
   638→                response = (
   639→                    f"**[Auto-reply {ar_config['turn_count']}/{ar_config['max_turns']}]:** "
   640→                    f"{event_text}\n\n---\n\n{response}"
   641→                )
   642→
   643→        assert f"**[Auto-reply 3/{_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS}]:**" in response
   644→        assert "Generated question" in response
   645→        assert "Agent response here" in response
   646→
   647→    def test_no_label_for_regular_messages(self):
   648→        """Regular messages are not labeled."""
   649→        response = "Agent response"
   650→        is_autoreply = False
   651→
   652→        if is_autoreply and response:
   653→            response = "SHOULD NOT HAPPEN"
   654→
   655→        assert response == "Agent response"
   656→

962→# ============================================================================
   963→# _CLIRunner — lightweight shim so ControlAPI can talk to the CLI
   964→# ============================================================================
   965→
   966→
   967→class _CLIRunner:
   968→    """Adapter between ControlAPI and HermesCLI.
   969→
   970→    Exposes the same interface that ControlAPI expects from GatewayRunner,
   971→    bridging the sync/single-session CLI with the async ControlAPI server.
   972→    """
   973→
   974→    def __init__(self, agent, session_id, cli_instance):
   975→        self._running_agents = {session_id: agent}
   976→        self._cli = cli_instance
   977→
   978→    def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True):
   979→        """Inject a message into the CLI's input queues."""
   980→        if interrupt and self._cli._agent_running:
   981→            self._cli._interrupt_queue.put(text)
   982→        else:
   983→            self._cli._pending_input.put(text)
   984→
   985→    def get_session_info(self, key: str) -> dict:
   986→        """Return session state for the control API."""
   987→        from agent.autoreply import session_info
   988→        cfg = getattr(self._cli, "_autoreply_config", None)
   989→        return session_info(cfg)
   990→
   991→
   992→# ============================================================================
   993→# HermesCLI Class
   994→# ============================================================================
   995→
   996→class HermesCLI:
   997→    """
   998→    Interactive CLI for the Hermes Agent.
   999→    
  1000→    Provides a REPL interface with rich formatting, command history,
  1001→    and tool execution capabilities.

1494→    def _start_control_api(self):
  1495→        """Start the control API in a background thread for external model switching."""
  1496→        if os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_API", "").lower() not in ("1", "true", "yes"):
  1497→            return
  1498→        try:
  1499→            import asyncio as _aio
  1500→            import threading
  1501→            from gateway.control_api import ControlAPI
  1502→
  1503→            shim = _CLIRunner(self.agent, self.session_id, self)
  1504→            api = ControlAPI(shim)
  1505→            ready = threading.Event()
  1506→
  1507→            def _run():
  1508→                try:
  1509→                    loop = _aio.new_event_loop()
  1510→                    _aio.set_event_loop(loop)
  1511→                    loop.run_until_complete(api.start())
  1512→                    ready.set()
  1513→                    loop.run_forever()
  1514→                except Exception as e:
  1515→                    logger.error("Control API thread crashed: %s", e)
  1516→                    ready.set()  # Unblock caller even on failure
  1517→
  1518→            t = threading.Thread(target=_run, daemon=True, name="control-api")
  1519→            t.start()
  1520→            # Clean up port file on exit (daemon thread won't call api.stop())
  1521→            import atexit
  1522→            atexit.register(ControlAPI._remove_port_file)
  1523→            ready.wait(timeout=5.0)
  1524→        except Exception as e:
  1525→            logger.debug("Control API failed to start: %s", e)
  1526→
  1527→    def show_banner(self):
  1528→        """Display the welcome banner in Claude Code style."""
  1529→        self.console.clear()
  1530→        if self.preloaded_skills and not self._startup_skills_line_shown:

  1532→            self.console.print(
  1533→                f"[bold {_accent_hex()}]Activated skills:[/] {skills_label}"

## Sense-Check Report: gateway/run.py Auto-Reply Implementation

**ISSUES FOUND:**

### 1. **RACE CONDITION in _generate_autoreply (Line 2519)**
   - **Issue**: Uses `del` to remove config while async LLM call may be in-flight
   - **Context**: At line 2519, when cap is reached, the code does `del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]` synchronously. However, by the time this executes, the _process_autoreply task (created at line 1974) may already have a reference to the config object. The next real user message (line 1829) will check `if session_key in self._autoreply_configs` but the value was deleted asynchronously.
   - **Actual behavior**: The next _process_autoreply task after cap-reached may still try to use a stale reference, OR _process_autoreply checks config existence at line 2560 and bails silently. This is defensive but creates unclear state.
   - **Better fix**: Use `.pop()` for consistency (like line 2500, 2064) to be more explicit.

### 2. **POTENTIAL RACE in turn counter reset (Line 1830)**
   - **Issue**: Modifies config dict without atomicity guarantee
   - **Context**: At line 1829-1830, code checks `if session_key in self._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply` then sets `["turn_count"] = 0`. Meanwhile, _process_autoreply can spawn fire-and-forget tasks that may modify the same config dict.
   - **Risk**: If _process_autoreply is running (called at line 1975 with `asyncio.create_task`), and a new user message arrives just after the agent finishes, there's a narrow window where both the reset and _generate_autoreply's `check_and_advance` (line 2517) can touch the config simultaneously.
   - **Mitigation already present**: `check_and_advance` is from `agent.autoreply` module which presumably handles the dict mutations; but confirm it's not reading stale state.

### 3. **Inconsistent cleanup pattern (Lines 2014, 2064, 2500, 2519)**
   - **Line 2014 (_handle_reset_command)**: Uses `.pop()`
   - **Line 2064 (_handle_stop_command)**: Uses `.pop()`
   - **Line 2500 (_handle_autoreply_command)**: Uses `.pop()`
   - **Line 2519 (_generate_autoreply)**: Uses `del` ← **INCONSISTENCY**
   - **Impact**: Minor, but `del` can raise KeyError if config was already removed; `.pop(session_key, None)` is safer.

### 4. **Incomplete error handling in _process_autoreply (Lines 2544-2561)**
   - **Issue**: Re-checks config existence at line 2560 after generating reply, but doesn't re-check before the adapter.send() at line 2547-2551.
   - **Scenario**: If user disables autoreply (line 2500) between lines 2541-2542 and 2544, the cap-reached message may still send even though autoreply was manually disabled.
   - **Current protection**: Minimal—relies on the config not being removed while this task runs, but _handle_stop_command can remove it (line 2064) anytime.

### 5. **Inconsistency: _handle_message doesn't reset turn_count for manually-disabled autoreply**
   - **Issue**: At line 1829, turn counter only resets on real user messages if autoreply is still active. But if user ran `/autoreply off`, the counter isn't reset for next session.
   - **Impact**: Low—the config is gone, so there's no counter to reset. But logically inconsistent.

---

## Summary
- **Real issue**: Line 2519 uses `del` instead of `.pop()` — inconsistent with other cleanup sites
- **Potential race**: Lines 1829-1830 and concurrent _process_autoreply tasks touching config dict, but mitigated by defensive checks at line 2560
- **Error handling**: Could strengthen re-checks in _process_autoreply before sending adapter messages (lines 2547-2551)

**Recommendation**: Change line 2519 from `del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]` to `self._autoreply_configs.pop(session_key, None)` for consistency and safety. The race conditions are tight and existing defensive checks (line 2560) mitigate them adequately.

## Final Sense-Check Report

### Issues Found:

1. **Line 1221 - Initialization duplication**: `_autoreply_config` is initialized in `__init__` (line 1221), but then the entire run() method reinitializes queues starting at line 4920. However, `_autoreply_config` is **not** reset in the run() method, which could cause stale config from a previous session to persist.

2. **Line 5924-5925 - Potential dict mutation race condition**: 
   ```python
   if self._autoreply_config and not user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
       self._autoreply_config["turn_count"] = 0
   ```
   No synchronization here. The control API's `_CLIRunner` thread (background) can read/write `_autoreply_config` via `get_session_info()` (line 988) while the main thread mutates it. Since dicts are not thread-safe for concurrent reads/writes, this could cause crashes or silent data corruption.

3. **Line 5958-5971 - Check-then-use pattern without re-check**:
   ```python
   if self._autoreply_config:
       reply = self._generate_autoreply_text()
       if reply:
           cfg = self._autoreply_config  # could be None here if cleared by interrupt
           turn = cfg["turn_count"]
   ```
   If an interrupt clears `self._autoreply_config` (line 4600) between the outer check and the inner assignment on line 5962, `cfg` becomes None and `cfg["turn_count"]` crashes.

4. **Line 5928 - Prefix stripping logic**:
   ```python
   if isinstance(user_input, str) and user_input.startswith(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):
       user_input = user_input[len(CLI_INPUT_PREFIX):]
   ```
   This assumes `user_input` is always a string, but line 5059 shows payloads can be tuples (text, images). The isinstance check catches this, but autoreply-generated replies (which get the prefix) passed via `_pending_input.put()` at line 5966 will be pure strings, so this works correctly **only by accident** — the logic is fragile.

5. **Line 980 - Thread safety in _CLIRunner.inject_message()**: 
   Reads `self._cli._agent_running` without synchronization. The main thread sets this flag at line 5931/5937. A race could cause the control API to route to the wrong queue. No mutex/lock guards this flag.

### Summary:

- **Most critical**: Line 5962 - dict access after None-check gap
- **High priority**: Line 5924-5925 and line 988 - unprotected dict mutation from two threads
- **Medium**: Line 980 - unsynchronized flag read
- **Low**: Line 1221 - config persistence across sessions (may be intentional)
- **Style issue**: Line 5928 - fragile tuple handling with prefix stripping

## Coverage Analysis Summary

### 1. **Public Functions in agent/autoreply.py - Coverage Status**

All 8 public functions are tested:
- ✓ `parse_autoreply_args` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `build_autoreply_messages` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `check_and_advance` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `prepare_llm_call` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `extract_reply` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `session_info` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `format_status` - Comprehensive unit tests
- ✓ `handle_command` - **TESTED via CLI/gateway integration, NOT isolated unit tests**

### 2. **Critical Gaps Found**

**Gap 1: `handle_command` has NO isolated unit tests**
- `handle_command` orchestrates `parse_autoreply_args`, state mutation, and user feedback
- Currently only tested through `_handle_autoreply_command` in CLI/gateway integration tests
- This means bugs in the state mutation logic (`current_config["max_turns"] = n`) aren't tested in isolation
- **Risk**: Integration tests may fail to catch logic errors in command handling

**Gap 2: `extract_reply` has a vacuously-true test**
- Test `test_whitespace_only_content` (line 338-347 in test_autoreply.py):
  ```python
  resp.choices[0].message.content = "   \n  "
  result = extract_reply(config, resp)
  assert result == ""  # ← PASSES but returns empty string, not None
  ```
- This test will **pass even if the code is broken**. The content is truthy, so `turn_count` increments, but then `.strip()` returns `""`. The test asserts the empty string is returned, but this is arguably a bug (should probably be `None` for empty stripped content).
- The actual code at line 182 (`return content.strip()`) returns the potentially-empty string unconditionally.

**Gap 3: Gateway `_generate_autoreply` doesn't verify `extract_reply` is called**
- Test at line 283-314 in `test_autoreply_command.py` mocks `async_call_llm` directly
- Never actually calls `extract_reply`, so can't detect if response parsing breaks
- The full code path uses `extract_reply` but the mock bypasses it

**Gap 4: CLI `_generate_autoreply_text` doesn't verify `extract_reply` is called**
- Test at line 143-170 in `test_cli_autoreply.py` mocks `call_llm` directly  
- Never actually calls `extract_reply`, so can't detect if response parsing breaks
- Same issue as gateway

**Gap 5: No edge case for `prepare_llm_call` with config containing extra keys**
- Current tests only verify standard config keys
- No test for forward compatibility if new keys are added to config

### 3. **What IS Tested Well**

- `parse_autoreply_args`: Comprehensive (aliases, flags, error cases, boundaries)
- `build_autoreply_messages`: Good coverage (history filtering, list flattening, truncation)
- `check_and_advance`: Good coverage (cap logic, literal mode, boundaries)
- Message injection and turn counting in CLI/gateway
- Error handling and cleanup on `/reset` and `/stop`
- Prefix stripping and turn counter reset logic

---

## Concise Summary

**Critical Issues:**
1. `handle_command` lacks isolated unit tests (only tested via integration)
2. `extract_reply`'s whitespace test is vacuously true (returns `""` but should maybe return `None`)
3. Gateway and CLI tests mock away `extract_reply` entirely — don't exercise real LLM response parsing

**Recommendation:** Add unit tests for `handle_command` directly, fix/clarify the whitespace handling in `extract_reply`, and consider removing mocks in integration tests to verify the full parsing path.

    62→
    63→**User config:** `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (settings), `~/.hermes/.env` (API keys)
    64→
    65→## File Dependency Chain
    66→
    67→```
    68→tools/registry.py  (no deps — imported by all tool files)
    69→       ↑
    70→tools/*.py  (each calls registry.register() at import time)
    71→       ↑
    72→model_tools.py  (imports tools/registry + triggers tool discovery)
    73→       ↑
    74→run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, environments/
    75→```
    76→
    77→---
    78→
    79→## AIAgent Class (run_agent.py)
    80→
    81→```python
    82→class AIAgent:
    83→    def __init__(self,
    84→        model: str = "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6",
    85→        max_iterations: int = 90,
    86→        enabled_toolsets: list = None,
    87→        disabled_toolsets: list = None,
    88→        quiet_mode: bool = False,
    89→        save_trajectories: bool = False,
    90→        platform: str = None,           # "cli", "telegram", etc.
    91→        session_id: str = None,
    92→        skip_context_files: bool = False,
    93→        skip_memory: bool = False,
    94→        # ... plus provider, api_mode, callbacks, routing params
    95→    ): ...
    96→
    97→    def chat(self, message: str) -> str:
    98→        """Simple interface — returns final response string."""
    99→
   100→    def run_conversation(self, user_message: str, system_message: str = None,
   101→                         conversation_history: list = None, task_id: str = None) -> dict:
   102→        """Full interface — returns dict with final_response + messages."""
   103→```
   104→
   105→### Agent Loop
   106→
   107→The core loop is inside `run_conversation()` — entirely synchronous:
   108→
   109→```python
   110→while api_call_count < self.max_iterations and self.iteration_budget.remaining > 0:
   111→    response = client.chat.completions.create(model=model, messages=messages, tools=tool_schemas)
   112→    if response.tool_calls:
   113→        for tool_call in response.tool_calls:
   114→            result = handle_function_call(tool_call.name, tool_call.args, task_id)
   115→            messages.append(tool_result_message(result))
   116→        api_call_count += 1
   117→    else:
   118→        return response.content
   119→```
   120→
   121→Messages follow OpenAI format: `{"role": "system/user/assistant/tool", ...}`. Reasoning content is stored in `assistant_msg["reasoning"]`.
   122→
   123→---
   124→
   125→## CLI Architecture (cli.py)
   126→
   127→- **Rich** for banner/panels, **prompt_toolkit** for input with autocomplete
   128→- **KawaiiSpinner** (`agent/display.py`) — animated faces during API calls, `┊` activity feed for tool results
   129→- `load_cli_config()` in cli.py merges hardcoded defaults + user config YAML
   130→- **Skin engine** (`hermes_cli/skin_engine.py`) — data-driven CLI theming; initialized from `display.skin` config key at startup; skins customize banner colors, spinner faces/verbs/wings, tool prefix, response box, branding text
   131→- `process_command()` is a method on `HermesCLI` (not in commands.py)
   132→- Skill slash commands: `agent/skill_commands.py` scans `~/.hermes/skills/`, injects as **user message** (not system prompt) to preserve prompt caching
   133→
   134→### Adding CLI Commands
   135→
   136→1. Add to `COMMANDS` dict in `hermes_cli/commands.py`
   137→2. Add handler in `HermesCLI.process_command()` in `cli.py`
   138→3. For persistent settings, use `save_config_value()` in `cli.py`
   139→
   140→---
   141→
   142→## Adding New Tools
   143→
   144→Requires changes in **3 files**:
   145→
   146→**1. Create `tools/your_tool.py`:**
   147→```python
   148→import json, os
   149→from tools.registry import registry
   150→
   151→def check_requirements() -> bool:
   152→    return bool(os.getenv("EXAMPLE_API_KEY"))
   153→
   154→def example_tool(param: str, task_id: str = None) -> str:
   155→    return json.dumps({"success": True, "data": "..."})
   156→
   157→registry.register(
   158→    name="example_tool",
   159→    toolset="example",
   160→    schema={"name": "example_tool", "description": "...", "parameters": {...}},
   161→    handler=lambda args, **kw: example_tool(param=args.get("param", ""), task_id=kw.get("task_id")),
   162→    check_fn=check_requirements,
   163→    requires_env=["EXAMPLE_API_KEY"],
   164→)
   165→```
   166→
   167→**2. Add import** in `model_tools.py` `_discover_tools()` list.
   168→
   169→**3. Add to `toolsets.py`** — either `_HERMES_CORE_TOOLS` (all platforms) or a new toolset.
   170→
   171→The registry handles schema collection, dispatch, availability checking, and error wrapping. All handlers MUST return a JSON string.
   172→
   173→**Agent-level tools** (todo, memory): intercepted by `run_agent.py` before `handle_function_call()`. See `todo_tool.py` for the pattern.
   174→
   175→---
   176→
   177→## Adding Configuration
   178→
   179→### config.yaml options:
   180→1. Add to `DEFAULT_CONFIG` in `hermes_cli/config.py`
   181→2. Bump `_config_version` (currently 5) to trigger migration for existing users
   182→
   183→### .env variables:
   184→1. Add to `OPTIONAL_ENV_VARS` in `hermes_cli/config.py` with metadata:
   185→```python
   186→"NEW_API_KEY": {
   187→    "description": "What it's for",
   188→    "prompt": "Display name",
   189→    "url": "https://...",
   190→    "password": True,
   191→    "category": "tool",  # provider, tool, messaging, setting
   192→},
   193→```
   194→
   195→### Config loaders (two separate systems):
   196→
   197→| Loader | Used by | Location |
   198→|--------|---------|----------|
   199→| `load_cli_config()` | CLI mode | `cli.py` |
   200→| `load_config()` | `hermes tools`, `hermes setup` | `hermes_cli/config.py` |
   201→| Direct YAML load | Gateway | `gateway/run.py` |
   202→
   203→---
   204→
   205→## Skin/Theme System
   206→
   207→The skin engine (`hermes_cli/skin_engine.py`) provides data-driven CLI visual customization. Skins are **pure data** — no code changes needed to add a new skin.
   208→
   209→### Architecture
   210→
   211→```
   212→hermes_cli/skin_engine.py    # SkinConfig dataclass, built-in skins, YAML loader
   213→~/.hermes/skins/*.yaml       # User-installed custom skins (drop-in)
   214→```
   215→
   216→- `init_skin_from_config()` — called at CLI startup, reads `display.skin` from config
   217→- `get_active_skin()` — returns cached `SkinConfig` for the current skin
   218→- `set_active_skin(name)` — switches skin at runtime (used by `/skin` command)
   219→- `load_skin(name)` — loads from user skins first, then built-ins, then falls back to default
   220→- Missing skin values inherit from the `default` skin automatically
   221→
   222→### What skins customize
   223→
   224→| Element | Skin Key | Used By |
   225→|---------|----------|---------|
   226→| Banner panel border | `colors.banner_border` | `banner.py` |
   227→| Banner panel title | `colors.banner_title` | `banner.py` |
   228→| Banner section headers | `colors.banner_accent` | `banner.py` |
   229→| Banner dim text | `colors.banner_dim` | `banner.py` |
   230→| Banner body text | `colors.banner_text` | `banner.py` |
   231→| Response box border | `colors.response_border` | `cli.py` |
   232→| Spinner faces (waiting) | `spinner.waiting_faces` | `display.py` |
   233→| Spinner faces (thinking) | `spinner.thinking_faces` | `display.py` |
   234→| Spinner verbs | `spinner.thinking_verbs` | `display.py` |
   235→| Spinner wings (optional) | `spinner.wings` | `display.py` |
   236→| Tool output prefix | `tool_prefix` | `display.py` |
   237→| Agent name | `branding.agent_name` | `banner.py`, `cli.py` |
   238→| Welcome message | `branding.welcome` | `cli.py` |
   239→| Response box label | `branding.response_label` | `cli.py` |
   240→| Prompt symbol | `branding.prompt_symbol` | `cli.py` |
   241→
   242→### Built-in skins
   243→
   244→- `default` — Classic Hermes gold/kawaii (the current look)
   245→- `ares` — Crimson/bronze war-god theme with custom spinner wings
   246→- `mono` — Clean grayscale monochrome
   247→- `slate` — Cool blue developer-focused theme
   248→
   249→### Adding a built-in skin
   250→
   251→Add to `_BUILTIN_SKINS` dict in `hermes_cli/skin_engine.py`:
   252→
   253→```python
   254→"mytheme": {
   255→    "name": "mytheme",
   256→    "description": "Short description",
   257→    "colors": { ... },
   258→    "spinner": { ... },
   259→    "branding": { ... },
   260→    "tool_prefix": "┊",
   261→},
   262→```
   263→
   264→### User skins (YAML)
   265→
   266→Users create `~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml`:
   267→
   268→```yaml
   269→name: cyberpunk
   270→description: Neon-soaked terminal theme
   271→
   272→colors:
   273→  banner_border: "#FF00FF"
   274→  banner_title: "#00FFFF"
   275→  banner_accent: "#FF1493"
   276→
   277→spinner:
   278→  thinking_verbs: ["jacking in", "decrypting", "uploading"]
   279→  wings:
   280→    - ["⟨⚡", "⚡⟩"]
   281→
   282→branding:
   283→  agent_name: "Cyber Agent"
   284→  response_label: " ⚡ Cyber "
   285→
   286→tool_prefix: "▏"
   287→```
   288→
   289→Activate with `/skin cyberpunk` or `display.skin: cyberpunk` in config.yaml.
   290→
   291→---
   292→
   293→## Important Policies
   294→### Prompt Caching Must Not Break
   295→
   296→Hermes-Agent ensures caching remains valid throughout a conversation. **Do NOT implement changes that would:**
   297→- Alter past context mid-conversation
   298→- Change toolsets mid-conversation
   299→- Reload memories or rebuild system prompts mid-conversation
   300→
   301→Cache-breaking forces dramatically higher costs. The ONLY time we alter context is during context compression.
   302→
   303→### Working Directory Behavior
   304→- **CLI**: Uses current directory (`.` → `os.getcwd()`)
   305→- **Messaging**: Uses `MESSAGING_CWD` env var (default: home directory)
   306→
   307→### Background Process Notifications (Gateway)
   308→
   309→When `terminal(background=true, check_interval=...)` is used, the gateway runs a watcher that
   310→pushes status updates to the user's chat. Control verbosity with `display.background_process_notifications`
   311→in config.yaml (or `HERMES_BACKGROUND_NOTIFICATIONS` env var):
   312→
   313→- `all` — running-output updates + final message (default)
   314→- `result` — only the final completion message
   315→- `error` — only the final message when exit code != 0
   316→- `off` — no watcher messages at all
   317→
   318→---
   319→
   320→## Known Pitfalls
   321→
   322→### DO NOT use `simple_term_menu` for interactive menus
   323→Rendering bugs in tmux/iTerm2 — ghosting on scroll. Use `curses` (stdlib) instead. See `hermes_cli/tools_config.py` for the pattern.
   324→
   325→### DO NOT use `\033[K` (ANSI erase-to-EOL) in spinner/display code
   326→Leaks as literal `?[K` text under `prompt_toolkit`'s `patch_stdout`. Use space-padding: `f"\r{line}{' ' * pad}"`.
   327→
   328→### `_last_resolved_tool_names` is a process-global in `model_tools.py`
   329→When subagents overwrite this global, `execute_code` calls after delegation may fail with missing tool imports. Known bug.
   330→
   331→### Tests must not write to `~/.hermes/`
   332→The `_isolate_hermes_home` autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py` redirects `HERMES_HOME` to a temp dir. Never hardcode `~/.hermes/` paths in tests.
   333→
   334→---
   335→
   336→## Testing
   337→
   338→```bash
   339→source .venv/bin/activate
   340→python -m pytest tests/ -q          # Full suite (~3000 tests, ~3 min)
   341→python -m pytest tests/test_model_tools.py -q   # Toolset resolution
   342→python -m pytest tests/test_cli_init.py -q       # CLI config loading
   343→python -m pytest tests/gateway/ -q               # Gateway tests
   344→python -m pytest tests/tools/ -q                 # Tool-level tests
   345→```
   346→
   347→Always run the full suite before pushing changes.
   348→
   349→---
   350→name: desloppify
   351→description: >
   352→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
   353→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
   354→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
   355→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
   356→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 29 languages.
   357→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
   358→---
   359→
   360→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
   361→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 6 -->
   362→
   363→# Desloppify
   364→
   365→## 1. Your Job
   366→
   367→Maximise the **strict score** honestly. Your main cycle: **scan → plan → execute → rescan**. Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
   368→
   369→**Don't be lazy.** Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy. If it takes touching 20 files, touch 20 files. If it's a one-line change, make it. No task is too big or too small — fix things properly, not minimally.
   370→
   371→## 2. The Workflow
   372→
   373→Three phases, repeated as a cycle.
   374→
   375→### Phase 1: Scan and review — understand the codebase
   376→
   377→```bash
   378→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
   379→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
   380→```
   381→
   382→The scan will tell you if subjective dimensions need review. Follow its instructions. To trigger a review manually:
   383→```bash
   384→desloppify review --prepare    # then follow your runner's review workflow
   385→```
   386→
   387→### Phase 2: Plan — decide what to work on
   388→
   389→After reviews, triage stages and plan creation appear in the execution queue surfaced by `next`. Complete them in order — `next` tells you what each stage expects in the `--report`:
   390→```bash
   391→desloppify next                                        # shows the next execution workflow step
   392→desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
   393→desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
   394→desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
   395→desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
   396→```
   397→
   398→For automated triage: `desloppify plan triage --run-stages --runner codex` (Codex) or `--runner claude` (Claude). Options: `--only-stages`, `--dry-run`, `--stage-timeout-seconds`.
   399→
   400→Then shape the queue. **The plan shapes everything `next` gives you** — `next` is the execution queue, not the full backlog. Don't skip this step.
   401→
   402→```bash
   403→desloppify plan                          # see the living plan details
   404→desloppify plan queue                    # compact execution queue view
   405→desloppify plan reorder <pat> top        # reorder — what unblocks the most?
   406→desloppify plan cluster create <name>    # group related issues to batch-fix
   407→desloppify plan focus <cluster>          # scope next to one cluster
   408→desloppify plan skip <pat>              # defer — hide from next
   409→```
   410→
   411→### Phase 3: Execute — grind the queue to completion
   412→
   413→Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue — finish the queue first.
   414→
   415→**Branch first.** Create a dedicated branch — never commit health work directly to main:
   416→```bash
   417→git checkout -b desloppify/code-health    # or desloppify/<focus-area>
   418→desloppify config set commit_pr 42        # link a PR for auto-updated descriptions
   419→```
   420→
   421→**The loop:**
   422→```bash
   423→# 1. Get the next item from the execution queue
   424→desloppify next
   425→
   426→# 2. Fix the issue in code
   427→
   428→# 3. Resolve it (next shows the exact command including required attestation)
   429→
   430→# 4. When you have a logical batch, commit and record
   431→git add <files> && git commit -m "desloppify: fix 3 deferred_import findings"
   432→desloppify plan commit-log record      # moves findings uncommitted → committed, updates PR
   433→
   434→# 5. Push periodically
   435→git push -u origin desloppify/code-health
   436→
   437→# 6. Repeat until the queue is empty
   438→```
   439→
   440→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
   441→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify autofix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
   442→
   443→**When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1.** New issues will surface, cascades will have resolved, priorities will have shifted. This is the cycle.
   444→
   445→## 3. Reference
   446→
   447→### Key concepts
   448→
   449→- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix → T2 quick manual → T3 judgment call → T4 major refactor.
   450→- **Auto-clusters**: related findings are auto-grouped in `next`. Drill in with `next --cluster <name>`.
   451→- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
   452→- **Wontfix cost**: widens the lenient↔strict gap. Challenge past decisions when the gap grows.
   453→
   454→### Scoring
   455→
   456→Overall score = **25% mechanical** + **75% subjective**.
   457→
   458→- **Mechanical (25%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
   459→- **Subjective (75%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
   460→- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
   461→- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
   462→
   463→### Reviews
   464→
   465→Four paths to get subjective scores:
   466→
   467→- **Local runner (Codex)**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — automated end-to-end.
   468→- **Local runner (Claude)**: `desloppify review --prepare` → launch parallel subagents → `desloppify review --import merged.json` — see skill doc overlay for details.
   469→- **Cloud/external**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → follow session template → `--external-submit`.
   470→- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
   471→
   472→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
   473→- Target-matching scores trigger auto-reset to prevent gaming. Use the blind-review workflow described in your agent overlay doc (e.g. `docs/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/HERMES.md`).
   474→- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
   475→- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.
   476→
   477→**Integrity rules:** Score from evidence only — no prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring. When evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty. Assess every requested dimension; never drop one.
   478→
   479→#### Review output format
   480→
   481→Return machine-readable JSON for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
   482→
   483→```json
   484→{
   485→  "session": {
   486→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
   487→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
   488→  },
   489→  "assessments": {
   490→    "<dimension_from_query>": 0
   491→  },
   492→  "findings": [
   493→    {
   494→      "dimension": "<dimension_from_query>",
   495→      "identifier": "short_id",
   496→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
   497→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
   498→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
   499→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
   500→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
   501→    }
   502→  ]
   503→}
   504→```
   505→
   506→`findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly (including `related_files`, `evidence`, and `suggestion`). Use `"findings": []` when no defects found. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal or cloud session imports. Legacy `--attested-external` remains supported.
   507→
   508→#### Import paths
   509→
   510→- Robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated prompt/template → run printed `--external-submit` command.
   511→- Durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
   512→- Findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
   513→
   514→#### Reviewer agent prompt
   515→
   516→Runners that support agent definitions (Cursor, Copilot, Gemini) can create a dedicated reviewer agent. Use this system prompt:
   517→
   518→```
   519→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
   520→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
   521→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
   522→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
   523→explain uncertainty.
   524→```
   525→
   526→See your editor's overlay section below for the agent config format.
   527→
   528→### Plan commands
   529→
   530→```bash
   531→desloppify plan reorder <cluster> top       # move all cluster members at once
   532→desloppify plan reorder <a> <b> top        # mix clusters + findings in one reorder
   533→desloppify plan reorder <pat> before -t X  # position relative to another item/cluster
   534→desloppify plan cluster reorder a,b top    # reorder multiple clusters as one block
   535→desloppify plan resolve <pat>              # mark complete
   536→desloppify plan reopen <pat>               # reopen
   537→desloppify backlog                          # broader non-execution backlog
   538→```
   539→
   540→### Commit tracking
   541→
   542→```bash
   543→desloppify plan commit-log                      # see uncommitted + committed status
   544→desloppify plan commit-log record               # record HEAD commit, update PR description
   545→desloppify plan commit-log record --note "why"  # with rationale
   546→desloppify plan commit-log record --only "smells::*"  # record specific findings only
   547→desloppify plan commit-log history              # show commit records
   548→desloppify plan commit-log pr                   # preview PR body markdown
   549→desloppify config set commit_tracking_enabled false  # disable guidance
   550→```
   551→
   552→After resolving findings as `fixed`, the tool shows uncommitted work, committed history, and a suggested commit message. After committing externally, run `record` to move findings from uncommitted to committed and auto-update the linked PR description.
   553→
   554→### Quick reference
   555→
   556→```bash
   557→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 execution items
   558→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
   559→desloppify backlog --count 5                      # top 5 backlog items outside execution
   560→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
   561→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
   562→desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" --attest "..." # accept debt
   563→desloppify exclude <path>                         # exclude a directory from scanning
   564→desloppify config show                            # show all config including excludes
   565→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
   566→```
   567→
   568→## 4. Fix Tool Issues Upstream
   569→
   570→When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent — a bug, a bad detection, a crash, confusing output — **fix it and open a PR**. If you can't confidently fix it, file an issue instead.
   571→
   572→### Fix and PR (preferred)
   573→
   574→Clone the tool repo to a temp directory, make the fix there, and verify it works against the project you're scanning before pushing.
   575→
   576→```bash
   577→git clone https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git /tmp/desloppify-fix
   578→cd /tmp/desloppify-fix
   579→git checkout -b fix/<short-description>
   580→```
   581→
   582→Make your changes, then run the test suite and verify the fix against the original project:
   583→
   584→```bash
   585→python -m pytest desloppify/tests/ -q
   586→python -m desloppify scan --path <project-root>   # the project you were scanning
   587→```
   588→
   589→Once it looks good, push and open a PR:
   590→
   591→```bash
   592→git add <files> && git commit -m "fix: <what and why>"
   593→git push -u origin fix/<short-description>
   594→gh pr create --title "fix: <short description>" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
   595→## Problem
   596→<what went wrong — include the command and output>
   597→
   598→## Fix
   599→<what you changed and why>
   600→EOF
   601→)"
   602→```
   603→
   604→Clean up after: `rm -rf /tmp/desloppify-fix`
   605→
   606→### File an issue (fallback)
   607→
   608→If the fix is unclear or the change needs discussion, open an issue at `https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify/issues` with a minimal repro: command, path, expected output, actual output.
   609→
   610→## Prerequisite
   611→
   612→`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`
   613→
   614→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   615→
   616→## Gemini CLI Overlay
   617→
   618→Gemini CLI has experimental subagent support, but subagents currently run
   619→sequentially (not in parallel). Review dimensions one at a time.
   620→
   621→### Setup
   622→
   623→Enable subagents in Gemini CLI settings:
   624→```json
   625→{
   626→  "experimental": {
   627→    "enableAgents": true
   628→  }
   629→}
   630→```
   631→
   632→Optionally define a reviewer agent in `.gemini/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
   633→
   634→```yaml
   635→---
   636→name: desloppify-reviewer
   637→description: Scores subjective codebase quality dimensions for desloppify
   638→kind: local
   639→tools:
   640→  - read_file
   641→  - search_code
   642→temperature: 0.2
   643→max_turns: 10
   644→---
   645→```
   646→
   647→Use the prompt from the "Reviewer agent prompt" section above.
   648→
   649→### Review workflow
   650→
   651→Invoke the reviewer agent for each group of dimensions sequentially.
   652→Even without parallelism, isolating dimensions across separate agent
   653→invocations prevents score bleed between concerns.
   654→
   655→Merge assessments and findings, then import.
   656→
   657→When Gemini CLI adds parallel subagent execution, split dimensions across
   658→concurrent agent calls instead.
   659→
   660→<!-- desloppify-overlay: gemini -->
   661→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   662→
   663→## Hermes Agent Overlay
   664→
   665→Hermes has built-in parallel subagent support via `delegate_task` (up to 3
   666→concurrent children). Use batch mode for review dimensions.
   667→
   668→### Review workflow
   669→
   670→1. **Prepare prompts:**
   671→```bash
   672→desloppify review --run-batches --dry-run
   673→```
   674→This generates per-batch prompt files in `.desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/`
   675→and prints the run directory path. Note the run directory — you need it for every step.
   676→
   677→2. **Launch subagents in groups of 3** using `delegate_task` with the `tasks` array.
   678→Each subagent reads its prompt file, inspects the repository, and writes its
   679→result. Subagents must not edit repository source files — only write the result.
   680→
   681→Output filename is `batch-N.raw.txt` (not `.json`) — the importer requires this exact name.
   682→
   683→Example `delegate_task` call:
   684→```
   685→delegate_task(tasks=[
   686→  {
   687→    "goal": "Review batch 1. Read the prompt at .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/batch-1.md, follow it exactly, inspect the repository, and write ONLY valid JSON to .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/results/batch-1.raw.txt.",
   688→    "context": "Repository root: <cwd>. Blind packet: .desloppify/review_packet_blind.json. The prompt file defines the required output schema. Do not edit repository source files. Only write the review result file.",
   689→    "toolsets": ["terminal", "file"]
   690→  },
   691→  {
   692→    "goal": "Review batch 2. Read the prompt at .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/batch-2.md, follow it exactly, inspect the repository, and write ONLY valid JSON to .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/results/batch-2.raw.txt.",
   693→    "context": "Repository root: <cwd>. Blind packet: .desloppify/review_packet_blind.json. The prompt file defines the required output schema. Do not edit repository source files. Only write the review result file.",
   694→    "toolsets": ["terminal", "file"]
   695→  },
   696→  {
   697→    "goal": "Review batch 3. Read the prompt at .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/batch-3.md, follow it exactly, inspect the repository, and write ONLY valid JSON to .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/results/batch-3.raw.txt.",
   698→    "context": "Repository root: <cwd>. Blind packet: .desloppify/review_packet_blind.json. The prompt file defines the required output schema. Do not edit repository source files. Only write the review result file.",
   699→    "toolsets": ["terminal", "file"]
   700→  }
   701→])
   702→```
   703→
   704→Repeat with batches 4-6, 7-9, etc. until all batches in the run are covered.
   705→Wait for each group of 3 to finish before launching the next.
   706→
   707→3. **Import results** (only after ALL batches for the run have results):
   708→```bash
   709→desloppify review --import-run .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id> --scan-after-import
   710→```
   711→
   712→The run directory must have results for every batch the dry-run created prompts
   713→for. If only a subset was run, generate a separate run for that subset instead.
   714→
   715→### Key constraints
   716→
   717→- `delegate_task` supports **max 3 concurrent children** — batch accordingly.
   718→- Subagents have **no parent context** — each prompt file is self-contained.
   719→- Subagents cannot call `delegate_task`, `clarify`, `memory`, or `send_message`.
   720→- Each subagent gets its own terminal session and file access.
   721→- Results must be **ONLY valid JSON** — the importer is strict.
   722→- Output filename must be `batch-N.raw.txt`, not `batch-N.json`.
   723→- The blind packet (`.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`) contains scan evidence
   724→  but no score history — this prevents anchoring bias.
   725→
   726→<!-- desloppify-overlay: hermes -->
   727→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   728→
   729→
```

1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes instance (CLI or gateway).
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
     7→
     8→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
     9→
    10→    # Send a message (interrupt mode — stops current work)
    11→    ctl.send_message("Focus on this instead")
    12→
    13→    # Queue a message (processed after current work finishes)
    14→    ctl.send_message("When you're done, also check logs", mode="queue")
    15→
    16→    # Send a slash command
    17→    ctl.send_message("/reset")
    18→    ctl.send_message("/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→    # List available commands
    24→    commands = ctl.list_commands()
    25→
    26→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    27→"""
    28→
    29→import json
    30→import os
    31→import urllib.request
    32→import urllib.error
    33→from typing import Optional
    34→
    35→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    36→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    37→
    38→
    39→class HermesControl:
    40→    """Client for the Hermes control API."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    43→        if port is None:
    44→            port = self._discover_port()
    45→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    46→
    47→    @staticmethod
    48→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    49→        try:
    50→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    51→                return int(f.read().strip())
    52→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    53→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    54→
    55→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    56→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    57→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    58→        headers = {"X-Hermes-Control": "1"}
    59→        if data:
    60→            headers["Content-Type"] = "application/json"
    61→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    62→            url,
    63→            data=data,
    64→            method=method,
    65→            headers=headers,
    66→        )
    67→        try:
    68→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    69→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    70→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    71→            return json.loads(e.read())
    72→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    73→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    74→
    75→    # ── Read-only endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────
    76→
    77→    def health(self) -> dict:
    78→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    79→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    80→
    81→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    82→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    83→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    84→
    85→    def get_session(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> dict:
    86→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    87→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    88→
    89→    def list_commands(self) -> dict:
    90→        """List available slash commands."""
    91→        return self._request("GET", "/commands")
    92→
    93→    # ── Message injection ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    94→
    95→    def send_message(
    96→        self,
    97→        text: str,
    98→        session_key: str = "_any",
    99→        mode: str = "interrupt",
   100→    ) -> dict:
   101→        """Send a message (or /command) into a running session.
   102→
   103→        Args:
   104→            text: Message text or slash command (e.g., "hello", "/reset").
   105→            session_key: Session to target. "_any" targets the first running session.
   106→            mode: "interrupt" (default) stops current work; "queue" waits for it to finish.
   107→        """
   108→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/message", {
   109→            "text": text,
   110→            "mode": mode,
   111→        })
   112→
   113→    # ── Convenience methods ────────────────────────────────────────────
   114→
   115→    def switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **kw) -> dict:
   116→        """Switch model via /model command."""
   117→        return self.send_message(f"/model {provider}:{model}", **kw)
   118→
   119→    def compact_context(self, **kw) -> dict:
   120→        """Trigger context compaction."""
   121→        return self.send_message("/compact", **kw)
   122→
   123→    def reset(self, **kw) -> dict:
   124→        """Reset conversation history."""
   125→        return self.send_message("/reset", **kw)
   126→
   127→    def stop(self, **kw) -> dict:
   128→        """Stop the running agent."""
   129→        return self.send_message("/stop", **kw)
   130→
   131→    def is_autoreply_enabled(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> bool:
   132→        """Check if autoreply is enabled for a session."""
   133→        info = self.get_session(session_key)
   134→        return bool(info.get("autoreply", {}).get("enabled"))
   135→
   136→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   137→        """Check if a Hermes instance with control API is reachable."""
   138→        try:
   139→            return self.health().get("status") == "ok"
   140→        except Exception:
   141→            return False
   142→
```
